## Binding register and honorification inventory

| Speaker → listener / subject | Required register or form | Why it is appropriate | Slices |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Adult learner → unfamiliar adult | Ordinary polite `해요체`; `저` only when useful | Neutral first contact without excessive deference or intimacy. | 1–25 |
| Adult peer/classmate/colleague → adult peer | Ordinary polite `해요체`; `이름+씨` only when natural | Polite relationship not established as intimate. | 1–2, 7, 9–10, 17–23, 25 |
| Student → teacher; teacher is clause subject | Ordinary polite ending plus subject `-시-`; title `선생님` | The respected teacher, not merely the listener, is the grammatical subject. | 24 |
| Student → teacher; learner requests teacher's action | Courteous `-아/어 주세요` with a clear task | The speech act is a request, not a third-person honorific statement. | 9, 20, 25 |
| Customer → routine service staff | Ordinary polite request or question; role/title only if natural | Staff interaction needs politeness but not elaborate business language. | 8, 11–13, 19–23, 25 |
| Service staff → customer; addressee performs an instructed action | Limited `-(으)세요` with a recoverable service task; `손님` or `고객님` only when natural | The customer is the understood actor in an instruction, not an honored third person in a report. | 12, 20, 24–25 |
| Service staff → coworker; customer or guest is clause subject | Ordinary polite service language with justified subject `-시-`; `손님` when natural | The customer or guest is appropriately honored as the subject of a report; no broad service script. | 24 |
| Younger adult → adult relative; older family member is subject | Ordinary polite family speech plus subject `-시-`; respectful kin term where natural | Relationship warrants subject honorification without formal workplace style. | 24–25 |
| Speaker → parent or older relative about sleep, eating, presence, age, or words | Limited `주무시다`, `드시다`, `계시다`, `연세`, `말씀` | These are the essential lexical honorifics assigned to this stage. A respectful age question uses `연세` and justified subject honorification; the numeric unit `세` alone does not make an age expression respectful. | 24–25 |
| Speaker asking or recording a respected person's name | `성함` with `-시-` where the respected person is subject | Respectful personal-information task; no full honorific conversion table. | 24 |
| Adult speaking to another adult about a child or close family member | Ordinary polite `해요체` toward the current adult listener | The deck can discuss close relationships without modeling an unnatural child-directed style or opening a casual speech-level system. | 3, 7, 16, 21–23 |
| Learner using Korean 00 conventional formulas | Exact inherited formula only, as support | Fixed formal endings are conventional chunks, not productive `합니다체`. | 25 |

Relationship context must be recoverable in every card involving a person whenever speech level, title, address, or honorification could change the answer. Self-honorification, blanket listener honorification, and random mixing of ordinary and formal polite styles are rejection grounds.

## Slice plan and prerequisite graph

### Rules shared by all slices

- Generate exactly the stated 40 IDs. The four primary-focus quotas in each slice are disjoint and sum to 40; a card may reinforce secondary features but is counted under only one quota.
- Start a new no-context generation session with the complete issue, this matrix, the exact slice, Korean 00, the current accepted Korean 01 target index, and the JSON contract. Start a separate no-context Korean-editor review for the completed batch.
- Introduce no predeclared answer list. Slice domains and collocation families guide generation but become inventory entries only after acceptance.
- Use only Korean 00 and accepted material from declared earlier Korean 01 slices as support. Material merely planned in an earlier slice is not a prerequisite until accepted.
- Keep one focused target, enough visible context, and a complete fair audio sentence. `sentence` contains `cloze` exactly once; `answer` equals `cloze`; terminal punctuation stays outside both.
- A cloze never crosses punctuation or a speaker-turn boundary. Hide a whole utterance only when it is a compact fixed high-value expression.
- Keep `accepted_answers` empty unless the independent editor confirms a genuine standard alternative. Never accept a connected-pronunciation spelling as an alternative.
- Do not add `reading` for Korean. Include optional `grammar`, `register`, or `note` fields only when directly useful, and never as empty strings.
- Audit grammar, naturalness, particles, inflection, spacing, speech level, honorification, numbers, counters, cloze fairness, and exact/normalized/semantic/mechanical duplication before accepting the slice.
- Standard-spelling recovery is required in all sound-change contexts. No phonetic answer or alternative is accepted.
- Replace each draft batch with the independently corrected JSON, run the existing source validation, then generate several local complete-sentence audio files with the repository's fixed Korean model, voice, and language configuration unchanged. Correct concrete language or synthesis failures before starting the next slice; do not commit audio.
- Store no generation prompt, review transcript, review state, or auxiliary workflow artifact.

### Slice 1 — identity, copula, topic, and focus (`ko-01-0001`–`ko-01-0040`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 14 copula/particle contrasts + 12 identity functions/collocations + 8 listening/orthography targets + 6 contextual contrast checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: productive `이에요/예요`, negative copula `아니에요`, first `은/는` versus `이/가`, ordinary present questions and answers, and natural subject omission; introduce and correct basic identity information.
- Domains and collocations: names, countries, nationalities, hometowns, and basic personal identity; stating identity, asking for identity, supplying new information, and correcting mistaken identity. These are candidate families, not accepted target strings.
- Numbers/counters: none; do not use arbitrary ages, dates, or identifiers before their assigned slices.
- Spelling/pronunciation: copula attachment after vowel- and consonant-final nouns, resyllabification across attached particles, phrase grouping in statements versus questions, and standard spacing.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: unfamiliar adults at a first meeting use ordinary polite speech; `저` is available when contrast requires it; names or titles replace repeated pronouns. Korean 00 conventional formal chunks may appear only as inherited support.
- Listening: distinguish identification, correction, topic, and focused-answer readings from the full situation and final contour.
- Production: type a novel identity statement, question, or correction with the correct copula and particle without copying a Korean 00 self-introduction string.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: none.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially direct Hangul input, self-introduction formulas as fixed wholes, copular chunks as unanalyzed prerequisites, spacing, and statement/question contours.
- Leakage exclusions: no productive formal style, nationality list padding, possession, number system, tense contrast, honorific paradigm, or Korean 00 target reuse.

### Slice 2 — language, occupation, study, and noun relations (`ko-01-0041`–`ko-01-0080`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 12 particle/construction targets + 14 study/work functions and collocations + 8 listening/orthography targets + 6 omission/coordination checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: consolidate copula; introduce `도`, `만`, `와/과`, spoken `하고` and `(이)랑`, natural `의`, high-frequency present `하다`, and omission of recoverable topics; exchange language, occupation, workplace, and study information.
- Domains and collocations: languages, school subjects, occupations, workplaces, courses, studying, working, and speaking; coordinate two nouns, add shared information, and restrict a choice.
- Numbers/counters: none; a visible course label may support context but cannot become a numeric series.
- Spelling/pronunciation: `하다` contraction to standard polite forms, particle attachment, noun-coordination phrase grouping, plain/aspirated/tense consonants in accepted domain words, and contextual vowel spelling.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: new adult classmates or colleagues use ordinary polite speech; `이름+씨` only among similar-status adults; do not force `의` where Korean uses a compound or omission.
- Listening: hear coordination, addition, restriction, and omitted repeated topics in short exchanges.
- Production: state or ask about work/study/language with a natural particle and one independently useful collocation.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slice 1.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially compound-vowel spelling, fixed questions, phrase grouping, and keyboard correction.
- Leakage exclusions: no counter, recipient-particle, tense-series, productive noun modification, formal style, or dictionary-headword list.

### Slice 3 — family, relationships, possession, people, and age (`ko-01-0081`–`ko-01-0120`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 12 possession/topic constructions + 12 family/relationship functions + 10 number/counter targets + 6 listening/pragmatic contrasts = 40.
- Grammar and functions: possession with `있어요/없어요`, `은/는` versus `이/가`, `하고` accompaniment, natural possession with or without `의`, and lexicalized family-description chunks; introduce family, say who lives or is together, and state ordinary age.
- Domains and collocations: immediate family, siblings, relatives, friends, household members, pets, living together, having a sibling or pet, and ordinary age statements.
- Numbers/counters: native people `명`, animals `마리`, and age `살`; required counter contractions and useful values across real family scenes, never a value-substitution series.
- Spelling/pronunciation: particles after 받침, resyllabification, nasalization in accepted family vocabulary, counter spacing, and phrase grouping for age and group size.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: ordinary polite family discussion with an adult peer; kinship terms are natural references; older family members can be identified, but productive `-시-` waits until slice 24.
- Listening: distinguish possession from bare existence and identify person, animal, and age counters.
- Production: describe a family or possession and give one practical age or household count with correct number form and spacing.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–2.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially complex 받침, connected pronunciation, first-meeting relationship cues, and fixed existence forms as sound/spelling prerequisites only.
- Leakage exclusions: no respectful age system yet, no broad kinship honorific conversion, no noun-modifier lesson, no family-name padding, and no repeated `있어요` frame with substituted nouns.

### Slice 4 — home, objects, existence, and static location (`ko-01-0121`–`ko-01-0160`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 14 existence/location/particle contrasts + 12 home-domain functions + 8 number/orthography targets + 6 listening/focus checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: `있어요/없어요` for existence versus location, static `에`, existential `이/가`, topic/contrast `은/는`, productive basic deictic forms, and location questions; locate people and objects, describe rooms, and state lack or presence.
- Domains and collocations: rooms, furniture, appliances, belongings, visible or previously mentioned objects, inside/outside, above/below, front/back, beside/between, and near/far in a recoverable home location.
- Numbers/counters: general objects `개` in practical household inventory; native counter forms and spacing.
- Spelling/pronunciation: resyllabification before particles, simple and common complex 받침, nasalization and tensification in accepted household words, and stable spelling under connected delivery.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult resident speaking to a guest, housemate, or service contact in ordinary polite speech; distinguish “there is one” from “it is at that place.”
- Listening: use entity status and location phrase to distinguish existence from location and topic from new-information subject.
- Production: ask or state where a person/object is or whether one exists, with correct `에`, particle, and optional object count.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–3.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially location chunks, 받침, resyllabification, standard-spelling recovery, and question contour.
- Leakage exclusions: no action-location `에서` before slice 7, no placement-result system, no exhaustive spatial noun list, and no mechanical room/object substitution.

### Slice 5 — calendar, weekdays, dates, and appointments (`ko-01-0161`–`ko-01-0200`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 10 time-particle/schedule constructions + 12 calendar functions + 12 number/date targets + 6 listening/orthography checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: time `에`, first `부터/까지`, calendar questions with `언제`, `무슨`, and `몇`, topic continuity, and present schedule statements; ask and answer dates, weekdays, and appointment availability.
- Domains and collocations: weekdays, months, years, appointment dates, school/work calendars, opening and closing days, birthdays only where relevant, and making or checking a basic appointment.
- Numbers/counters: Sino year `년`, month `월`, date `일`, named weekdays, numeric duration `일`, native duration `주/달`, and the lexical short-day forms `하루/이틀/사흘/나흘`; include `유월` and `시월` as standard month forms and meaningful contemporary dates rather than arbitrary series.
- Spelling/pronunciation: calendar-unit spacing, connected grouping of full dates, lateralization and tensification in accepted calendar words, and question versus confirmation contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adults arranging a routine appointment with a classmate, colleague, or service desk; corrections must identify which detail is wrong.
- Listening: distinguish weekday from numeric date, event time from range, and information question from confirmation.
- Production: give, ask for, correct, or confirm a real calendar detail with the proper system and unit.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–4.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially the fixed appointment-time confirmation as support only, number/place confirmation contour, lateralization, and punctuation.
- Leakage exclusions: no systematic before/after construction, no long calendar recital, no date substitution padding, and no future form before slice 18.

### Slice 6 — clock time, frequency, duration, and routine schedules (`ko-01-0201`–`ko-01-0240`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 10 schedule/range constructions + 10 routine functions and collocations + 14 number/time targets + 6 listening/spacing contrasts = 40.
- Grammar and functions: native-hour and Sino-minute combination, `반`, `부터/까지`, time `에`, recurring present, and `만` in practical scheduling; tell exact time, opening hours, frequency, and duration.
- Domains and collocations: waking, meals, school/work start and finish, breaks, appointments, operating hours, weekly routines, waiting, and travel duration as schedule information.
- Numbers/counters: clock `시`, minute `분`, half `반`, occurrence `번`, meals `끼`, and duration `시간/분/주/달`; explicitly contrast clock hour `시` with duration `시간` and exact minute with duration through context.
- Spelling/pronunciation: native contractions before `시`, counter spacing, ordinary boundary tensification, phrase grouping around `부터/까지`, and stable standard spelling.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adults comparing schedules or a customer asking opening time; frequency answers must solve a real question rather than enumerate numbers.
- Listening: recover hour/minute system, exact time versus duration, and range start versus endpoint.
- Production: state or ask one schedule, frequency, or duration with correct number system, counter, particle, and spacing.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–5.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially time-question rhythm, fixed time confirmation as support, phrase boundaries, and connected spelling.
- Leakage exclusions: no arbitrary clock table, no before/after system, no limit/comparison construction, and no digit-only targets.

### Slice 7 — daily routine, objects, and action location (`ko-01-0241`–`ko-01-0280`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 14 present/action/particle targets + 12 routine collocations + 8 listening/orthography targets + 6 omission/frequency checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: ordinary polite action present, `을/를`, action location `에서`, time `에`, high-frequency `하다`, subject/object omission, `도`, and established frequency; describe a normal day and recurring actions.
- Domains and collocations: getting up, washing, eating, leaving, studying, working, exercising, shopping, returning home, resting, and sleeping, each as an independently useful collocation.
- Numbers/counters: established time and occurrence `번` only when the routine requires them; no new counter.
- Spelling/pronunciation: common polite contractions, `하다` forms, particles after finals, aspiration and tensification in accepted routine verbs, and ordinary phrase grouping.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult classmate, colleague, or family conversation remains polite; omit the repeated first-person subject after topic establishment.
- Listening: distinguish object from subject, time `에` from action place `에서`, and explicit from omitted arguments.
- Production: describe one recurring action with its natural object, time, or place, without building a repeated daily-schedule template.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–6.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially keyboard control, request and classroom vocabulary only as support, sound changes, and statement contour.
- Leakage exclusions: no past, future, clause sequence, obligation, ability, or systematic before/after language; no routine-verb headword list.

### Slice 8 — transport, destinations, sources, direction, and means (`ko-01-0281`–`ko-01-0320`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 14 motion/particle contrasts + 10 transport functions and collocations + 10 number/counter targets + 6 listening/direction checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: destination `에`, source/action place `에서`, direction or means `으로/로`, `부터/까지`, present motion, and location questions; ask how to get somewhere, select transport, and state origin, destination, and travel time.
- Domains and collocations: stations, stops, bus, subway, taxi, walking, boarding, leaving a source, reaching a destination, route selection, tickets, and simple left/right/straight service directions.
- Numbers/counters: route identifier `번`, vehicle `대`, ticket `장`, clock time and duration `시간/분`, distance `미터/킬로미터`, and broadly useful floor `층` when a destination is inside a station or building.
- Spelling/pronunciation: `으로/로` choice after a final `ㄹ`, counter spacing, route-number grouping, lateralization in accepted transport vocabulary, and question/request contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: traveler to station staff or another adult; shared destination may license omission, but the scene must distinguish source, endpoint, and means.
- Listening: distinguish `에`, `에서`, and `으로/로`; identify route number versus occurrence count and clock time versus travel duration.
- Production: ask or state one practical route, direction, origin/destination, means, or travel-time fact.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–7.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially fixed direction and station-confirmation chunks as support only, lateralization, request contour, and standard spacing.
- Leakage exclusions: no ability form, no complex travel narrative, no exhaustive address system, no arbitrary route-number series, and no systematic before/after directions.

### Slice 9 — classroom tasks, materials, and action requests (`ko-01-0321`–`ko-01-0360`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 12 object/action-request constructions + 12 classroom functions and collocations + 10 counter/orthography targets + 6 listening/request checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: `을/를`, action location `에서`, productive `-아/어 주세요`, high-frequency `하다`, information questions, and omitted shared objects; ask for classroom actions, ask and answer questions, and manage materials.
- Domains and collocations: lessons, courses, books, pages, paper, assignments, questions, answers, reading, writing, listening, speaking, opening, closing, borrowing, checking, and submitting.
- Numbers/counters: books `권`, flat items/pages `장`, ordinal `번째`, class time already established; every value supports a distinct classroom task.
- Spelling/pronunciation: request-form contraction and spacing before `주세요`, resyllabification before object particles, aspiration in accepted classroom verbs, counter spacing, and request versus information-question contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult learner to teacher or classmate in ordinary polite speech; the requested action and responsible listener must be clear; Korean 00 request strings cannot be reused as targets.
- Listening: distinguish a request for an action from a question or statement and recover omitted shared classroom objects.
- Production: make one novel useful classroom request or information move with a fair compact cloze and correct spacing.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–8.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially classroom/display words, fixed requests as unanalyzed support, phrase boundaries, punctuation, and sound-rule recovery.
- Leakage exclusions: no command paradigm, obligation, permission expansion, formal classroom style, long dialogue, or copied Korean 00 request with a substituted object.

### Slice 10 — workplace schedules, recipients, telephone, and messages (`ko-01-0361`–`ko-01-0400`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 12 recipient/contact constructions + 10 workplace/message functions + 12 number/identifier targets + 6 listening/repair checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: limited recipient `에게/한테`, `하고`, `의` where natural, `에서` source or action place, `으로/로` means, present `하다`, and action requests; exchange contact details, leave a basic message, and arrange work/study schedules.
- Domains and collocations: office or school contact, colleague/classmate, calling, sending, receiving, message, number, meeting room, floor, department, and checking one contact detail.
- Numbers/counters: telephone digits with `공`, room `호`, floor `층`, route/identifier `번`, ordinal `번째`, machine `대`, duration `개월`, and established date/time.
- Spelling/pronunciation: digit grouping, contextual `공` rather than `영`, spaces before units, particles after numbers or names, lateralization in accepted contact vocabulary, and confirmation contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult peer or routine receptionist interaction; use `한테` in ordinary speech and `에게` only where neutral wording is natural; no elaborate office hierarchy.
- Listening: recover grouped telephone digits, distinguish recipient from companion, and identify one corrected digit, room, floor, or time.
- Production: give, request, confirm, or correct one practical contact or schedule detail with correct grouping and particle.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–9.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially number confirmation as a fixed support pattern, audio repair, spelling under lateralization, and question/confirmation intonation.
- Leakage exclusions: no indirect quotation, formal telephone script, broad business honorifics, arbitrary telephone-number series, or database-like personal-information cards.

### Slice 11 — food, drinks, preferences, and noun requests (`ko-01-0401`–`ko-01-0440`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 12 preference/request constructions + 12 food functions and collocations + 10 number/counter targets + 6 listening/availability checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: descriptive present, natural preference predicates, `도` versus `만`, object marking, availability `있어요/없어요`, and noun `주세요`; state likes/dislikes, ask what is available, order an item, and make a simple choice.
- Domains and collocations: meals, staple foods, dishes, fruit, vegetables, drinks, taste, menu choices, eating and drinking, preferred and unavailable items.
- Numbers/counters: `개`, `잔`, `병`, `그릇`, and meal count `끼` with native number forms; established person count only if a reservation makes it relevant.
- Spelling/pronunciation: counter spacing, contractions in eating/drinking forms, resyllabification, nasalization and tensification in accepted food vocabulary, and choice/request intonation.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: customer to café or restaurant staff, or adults discussing preferences; a listener's preference question must be appropriate and not presume a choice.
- Listening: distinguish a product request from an availability question, addition `도` from restriction `만`, and like/dislike from simple action.
- Production: order or discuss one useful food/drink choice with a natural counter or preference collocation.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–10.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially service attention and packaging chunks as support only, complex finals, request contour, and connected pronunciation.
- Leakage exclusions: no comparative preference system, recipe narration, permission/obligation expansion, menu-item substitution series, or reuse of Korean 00 food targets.

### Slice 12 — restaurant service, quantities, and request contrasts (`ko-01-0441`–`ko-01-0480`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 14 request-form contrasts + 10 restaurant/service functions + 10 number/counter targets + 6 listening/register checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: contrast noun `주세요` with action `-아/어 주세요`; introduce limited service `-(으)세요` as invitation/direction; use `있어요/없어요` availability and short choice questions; order, request a service action, accept a seating direction, and check an order.
- Domains and collocations: table, seat, menu, party size, serving, water, utensil, order, bill, payment point, takeout or dine-in choice, and correcting a missing or wrong item.
- Numbers/counters: people `명`, servings `인분`, bowls `그릇`, glasses `잔`, bottles `병`, clock `시/분` for a reservation.
- Spelling/pronunciation: contrast spacing in noun requests and action requests, attach `-(으)세요`, preserve 받침 in counter phrases, and distinguish request, instruction, and confirmation contours.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: customer and routine restaurant staff; staff may give a simple seating invitation, while the customer requests an item or action. No broad service honorific system yet.
- Listening: identify requested object, requested action, staff direction, party count, and correction from the scene.
- Production: perform one restaurant move with the correct request family, counter, spacing, and relationship.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–11.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially service attention, request spacing, connected phrase delivery, and fixed permission/prohibition chunks as support only.
- Leakage exclusions: no productive permission, obligation, broad honorific service conversion, multi-turn script cloze, or quantity substitution padding.

### Slice 13 — shopping, descriptions, prices, and availability (`ko-01-0481`–`ko-01-0520`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 12 description/availability constructions + 10 shopping functions and collocations + 12 price/counter targets + 6 listening/orthography checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: descriptive present, noun `주세요`, `만`, `도`, availability `있어요/없어요`, object marking, and lexicalized product-description chunks; ask price, color, size, stock, and payment method, then choose or decline an item politely.
- Domains and collocations: clothing, shoes, bags, colors, sizes, everyday goods, cash/card payment, receipt, price, cheap/expensive, big/small, long/short, and in-stock/out-of-stock situations.
- Numbers/counters: currency `원`, clothing `벌`, pairs `켤레`, general items `개`, sheets/cards `장`, weight `그램/킬로그램`, and useful floor/room units for a department or fitting room.
- Spelling/pronunciation: price grouping through thousands and ten-thousands, counter spacing, common `ㅡ` and `ㅂ` alternations in accepted descriptive vocabulary, lexical vowel spelling, and question/confirmation contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult customer and shop staff use ordinary polite service language; restrictions with `만` must reflect a real choice, not a grammar demonstration.
- Listening: distinguish price from quantity, size from number, availability from possession, and descriptive predicate from noun identity.
- Production: ask, answer, choose, or pay in one shopping situation with correct number system, counter, description, and spacing.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–12.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially service requests, packaging as inherited support, vowel spelling, complex finals, and question intonation.
- Leakage exclusions: no comparison system, bargaining language, large-price recital, mechanically varied product catalog, or productive adjective modification.

### Slice 14 — consolidated quantities, counters, and practical measurement (`ko-01-0521`–`ko-01-0560`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 8 counter-selection constructions + 10 quantity interactions + 16 number/counter targets + 6 listening/error checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: consolidate native versus Sino selection, `몇` and `얼마`, `만`, `있어요/없어요`, noun requests, and counter-bearing noun phrases; ask, state, correct, and fulfill quantities without using a substitution drill.
- Domains and collocations: household inventory, group/reservation size, pets, books, tickets, food/drink orders, vehicles/devices, clothing, shopping weight, price, floor, room, and duration.
- Numbers/counters: binding consolidation of `개`, `명`, `마리`, `권`, `장`, `병`, `잔`, `대`, `벌`, `켤레`, `인분`, `그릇`, `끼`, `살`, `번`, `번째`, `층`, `호`, `시간`, `분`, numeric `일`, lexical `하루/이틀/사흘/나흘`, `주`, `달`, `개월`, `원`, `미터`, `킬로미터`, `그램`, and `킬로그램`; respectful `분` and formal/documentary age `세` remain for slice 24.
- Spelling/pronunciation: native counter contractions, counter spacing, ambiguous `분` resolved by context, phrase grouping for large prices and durations, and no spelling change from boundary sound processes.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: home, classroom, shop, restaurant, transport, and service scenes; each counter answers a distinct practical need and the scene identifies the referent.
- Listening: choose system and counter from referent/function, distinguish quantity from identifier, and catch one wrong counter or unit in a correction scene.
- Production: produce a practical quantity, correction, or measurement whose function is not already represented by a mechanical variant.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–13.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially number confirmation as support, final-consonant spelling, connected delivery, and local keyboard correction.
- Leakage exclusions: no number/counter engine, exhaustive measure list, arithmetic quiz, arbitrary numeral series, comparison/limit system, or respectful counter before its relationship context.

### Slice 15 — weather, seasons, temperature, and descriptive present (`ko-01-0561`–`ko-01-0600`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 14 descriptive/irregular targets + 10 weather/season functions + 10 number/orthography targets + 6 listening/state contrasts = 40.
- Grammar and functions: ordinary descriptive present, action versus descriptive predicates, topic/focus, first systematic high-frequency `ㅂ`, `르`, and `ㅡ` forms only in natural vocabulary, and lexicalized description chunks; describe weather, season, temperature, and a simple outing condition.
- Domains and collocations: hot/cold, warm/cool, clear/cloudy, rain/snow, wind, season, sky, temperature, clothing need, and a near-term outing decision stated in the present.
- Numbers/counters: temperature `도` with Sino values and `영` for zero; date/month and clock time only where a forecast or outing requires them.
- Spelling/pronunciation: accepted irregular polite forms, contractions, aspiration involving `ㅎ`, nasalization/tensification in weather vocabulary, temperature spacing, and statement versus weather-question contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adults discussing current conditions or a customer asking simple weather-dependent service information; avoid exaggerated forecast style.
- Listening: distinguish current weather state, temperature value, seasonal fact, and action affected by the weather.
- Production: describe one condition or temperature and make one immediate practical response with correct descriptive form.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–14.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially known weather spelling, `ㅎ` behavior, complex finals, contractions, and connected phrase delivery.
- Leakage exclusions: no productive future before slice 18, no reason connective before slice 22, no forecast report, no comparative climate statements, and no irregular-form table.

### Slice 16 — health and immediate basic needs (`ko-01-0601`–`ko-01-0640`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 12 descriptive/state constructions + 12 health/need functions and collocations + 8 duration/orthography targets + 8 listening/repair checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: present descriptive and state predicates, possession/existence for medicine or appetite, ordinary need and request chunks justified by the matrix, and natural high-frequency `르`, `ㅡ`, and selected `ㅂ` forms; state a symptom, ask for water/rest/help, and report basic condition.
- Domains and collocations: head, stomach, throat, fever, pain, tiredness, hunger, thirst, appetite, medicine, rest, sleep, and immediate non-specialist help.
- Numbers/counters: temperature `도`, duration `분/시간`, numeric-day `일` or a natural short-day form, meal count `끼`, and medicine quantity `개` only in a clear ordinary instruction or service context.
- Spelling/pronunciation: contractions in symptom predicates, aspiration and nasalization in accepted health vocabulary, particles after body terms, counter spacing, and calm request intonation.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult to family member, teacher, pharmacist, or routine service contact; communicate needs without diagnosing or using alarming specialist language.
- Listening: recover body location, current condition, duration, and requested basic assistance from a short scene.
- Production: state one immediate symptom or need and make or answer one ordinary assistance request.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–15.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially help/audio-repair chunks as support, complex finals, aspiration, and polite request contour.
- Leakage exclusions: no medical specialization, advice/obligation system, ability construction, conditionals, emergency script expansion, or symptom substitution list.

### Slice 17 — simple past events and past states (`ko-01-0641`–`ko-01-0680`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 16 past-form/tense contrasts + 10 event functions and collocations + 8 time/orthography targets + 6 listening/timeline checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: ordinary polite past for action, descriptive, copular, existential, and high-frequency irregular predicates; explicit present-versus-past contrast; ask and answer about one completed event or prior state.
- Domains and collocations: yesterday, last week, meal, class, work, shopping, short outing, transport, appointment, weather, and basic condition, each limited to one event or state.
- Numbers/counters: past dates, clock times, prices, durations `시간/분/일`, and quantities only when they identify a distinct event detail.
- Spelling/pronunciation: standard past contractions, `하다` past, accepted `ㅂ`, `ㄷ`, `르`, and `ㅡ` forms, resyllabification, tensification, and statement/question contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult peers, classmate/teacher, or customer/service contact; the time anchor must justify past rather than merely changing an ending.
- Listening: distinguish current routine/state from completed event/past state and recover the relevant time phrase.
- Production: report, ask about, correct, or confirm one simple past event or state with a natural collocation.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–16.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially past-looking fixed forms only as unanalyzed sound support, connected spelling, phrase grouping, and confirmation contour.
- Leakage exclusions: no connected narration, systematic before/after, experience construction, indirect quotation, or present-to-past mechanical pair series.

### Slice 18 — expected future, desire, invitations, and plans (`ko-01-0681`–`ko-01-0720`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 16 future/desire constructions + 10 invitation/plan functions + 8 schedule/number targets + 6 listening/time-reference checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: `-(으)ㄹ 거예요`, `-고 싶어요`, destination and means particles, established time/date language, and simple questions or invitations; state an expected or planned action, express desire, invite, accept, or decline briefly.
- Domains and collocations: weekend, seasonal outing, meal, shopping, study, work, appointment, transport, visit, weather-dependent plan, and choosing a destination or activity.
- Numbers/counters: future date, weekday, clock time, travel duration, ticket `장`, person `명`, and established route `번` only as necessary plan details.
- Spelling/pronunciation: space before `거예요` and `싶어요`, future form after vowel/final consonant, accepted irregular stems, lateralization in plan vocabulary, and invitation/question contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult peers or family speaking politely, customer arranging a service, or student discussing a plan with a teacher; invitations remain non-presumptive.
- Listening: distinguish present schedule from expected future and desire from a settled plan.
- Production: state or ask about one planned/expected action or desire and handle one concise invitation response.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–17.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially question/readiness contours as support, lateralization, complex finals, and standard spacing.
- Leakage exclusions: no `-(으)려고 하다`, conditions, obligation, attempt, experience, future noun-modifier system, or multi-event itinerary.

### Slice 19 — negation, inability, failure, and interaction repair (`ko-01-0721`–`ko-01-0760`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 16 polarity/negative contrasts + 10 repair/clarification functions + 8 number/contact checks + 6 listening/pragmatic distinctions = 40.
- Grammar and functions: `안` versus `못`, negative copula versus `없어요`, negated `하다`, present/past polarity where the situation motivates it, and clarification/confirmation questions; state nonperformance, inability, lack, wrong identity, and service or communication problems.
- Domains and collocations: missed transport, unavailable product or time, forgotten or incorrect contact detail, incomplete school/work task, food restriction, health limitation, and asking for clarification or repetition.
- Numbers/counters: correct one telephone digit, time, date, price, counter, or duration as an interactional repair; no arbitrary alternatives.
- Spelling/pronunciation: spaces before `안` and `못`, natural placement with `하다` collocations, contraction and connected delivery, negative-question contour, and standard spelling through sound change.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: calm ordinary repair with adult peer, teacher, staff, or service contact; distinguish choice, inability, lack, correction, and inherited prohibition.
- Listening: identify why an action did not occur and whether the answer expresses volition, inability, absence, or mistaken identity.
- Production: give one motivated negative or inability statement and one concise clarification/correction without invoking a later ability system.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–18.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially fixed negative/prohibition and comprehension-repair chunks as support only, audio repair, spacing, and question contour.
- Leakage exclusions: no productive ability, obligation, condition, excuse narrative, copied Korean 00 repair target, or polarity-only duplicate pair.

### Slice 20 — requests, instructions, invitations, and service directions (`ko-01-0761`–`ko-01-0800`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 18 request/instruction contrasts + 10 service/classroom functions + 6 orthography targets + 6 listening/actor checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: systematic operational contrast among noun `주세요`, action `-아/어 주세요`, and `-(으)세요`; limited recipient `에게/한테`; polite response and omission of a shared object; request, instruct, invite, direct, and respond.
- Domains and collocations: classroom tasks, seating, opening/closing, waiting, entering/leaving, showing/checking, giving an item to a recipient, restaurant/shop service, and simple route or safety directions.
- Numbers/counters: established order quantity, page/ticket number, route, time, or floor only when required by the instruction; no new number function.
- Spelling/pronunciation: space before `주세요`, attach `-(으)세요`, accepted irregular command/request stems, aspiration involving `ㅎ`, phrase boundaries, and request versus instruction contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: teacher/student, staff/customer, or unfamiliar adults; scene identifies the addressee as actor. A direct instruction must be appropriate, not a disguised command to a superior.
- Listening: identify requested item, requested action, instructed/invited action, recipient, and expected response.
- Production: choose one of the three forms for a justified speech act and produce it with correct spacing, actor, and politeness.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–19.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially fixed requests and service expressions as immutable support, punctuation boundaries, aspiration, and request intonation.
- Leakage exclusions: no obligation, broad imperative paradigm, productive permission/prohibition beyond inherited fixed chunks, self-honorific reading, or copied request expanded by one noun.

### Slice 21 — coordination and short sequence with `-고` (`ko-01-0801`–`ko-01-0840`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 16 `-고`/particle contrasts + 12 sequence functions and collocations + 6 listening/orthography targets + 6 discourse-boundary checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: `-고` for predicate coordination or short sequence, contrast with noun `하고`, natural shared subject omission, established tense at the final predicate, and connector `그리고`; join two closely related daily actions or descriptions.
- Domains and collocations: routine, meal, school/work, shopping, transport, contact, and service sequences limited to two short clauses or two short sentences.
- Numbers/counters: established time, quantity, or route may anchor a sequence, but no new number function and no digit variant series.
- Spelling/pronunciation: attach `-고`, distinguish it from spaced noun coordination, maintain standard stem spelling, group two clauses naturally, and keep each cloze inside one sentence boundary.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult peers or routine service contacts; omit a shared subject when recoverable and avoid repeated pronouns.
- Listening: hear noun coordination versus predicate linkage, clause boundary, shared subject, and event order.
- Production: link two independently useful, closely related actions or states with `-고`, or connect two short sentences with `그리고`.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–20.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially phrase grouping, punctuation boundaries, connected sound, and natural short-turn delivery.
- Leakage exclusions: no long narration, systematic before/after, `-고 싶어요` confusion, indirect quotation, or multi-clause cloze.

### Slice 22 — ordinary reason and linked sequence with `-아서/어서` (`ko-01-0841`–`ko-01-0880`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 16 cause/sequence constructions + 12 reason/repair functions + 6 listening/orthography targets + 6 connective contrasts = 40.
- Grammar and functions: `-아서/어서` for an ordinary cause/reason or natural tightly linked sequence, `그래서`, established `못`, particles, and subject omission; explain a daily choice, problem, route, absence, need, or result briefly.
- Domains and collocations: weather and plans, health and rest, transport delay, product availability, classroom/work problem, food choice, and service repair.
- Numbers/counters: an established time, duration, price, or route may supply the concrete reason; no new counter.
- Spelling/pronunciation: vowel harmony and standard contracted forms, accepted irregular stems, resyllabification, aspiration, phrase grouping around the connective, and reason-question contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: adult peers, teacher/student, or customer/staff; reasons stay factual and concise rather than argumentative or blameful.
- Listening: distinguish cause/tightly linked sequence from neutral addition and identify the result clause or following `그래서` sentence.
- Production: give one practical reason or tightly linked two-step event with natural `-아서/어서` or a two-sentence `그래서` relation.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–21.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially phrase boundaries, contractions, connected pronunciation, and calm repair pragmatics.
- Leakage exclusions: no conditions, obligation, attempt, extended explanation, causal chain, connected narration, or cloze crossing a clause-ending sentence boundary.

### Slice 23 — contrast with `-지만` and short connector control (`ko-01-0881`–`ko-01-0920`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 16 connective contrasts + 12 choice/explanation functions + 6 listening/orthography targets + 6 cumulative tense/polarity checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: `-지만`, contrast among `-고`, `-아서/어서`, and `-지만`, sentence connectors `그리고`, `그래서`, `그런데`, plus established present/past/future, `안/못`, and desire; contrast two options, acknowledge a limitation, or explain a changed choice.
- Domains and collocations: food, shopping, schedules, transport, weather, health, study/work, invitations, and service choices, limited to two short clauses or sentences.
- Numbers/counters: contrasting times, prices, sizes, quantities, routes, or durations only when the contrast drives a real choice; mechanical numeric pairs are rejected.
- Spelling/pronunciation: attach clause endings, preserve spaces around sentence connectors, maintain irregular stem spelling, group contrastive phrases, and distinguish statement from follow-up question.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: polite disagreement, refusal, or correction with an adult peer or service contact; avoid blunt criticism and unnecessary pronouns.
- Listening: identify whether the relation is addition, cause, or contrast and track tense/polarity on each side without requiring a long narrative.
- Production: produce one short motivated contrast or select the correct connector in a practical choice or repair scene.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–22.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially final contours, phrase grouping, standard spacing, and correction pragmatics.
- Leakage exclusions: no systematic comparison/limit, conditions, abstract argument, multi-event narration, or connector-only substitution set.

### Slice 24 — subject honorification and respectful personal interaction (`ko-01-0921`–`ko-01-0960`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 18 `-시-`/ordinary-form contrasts + 10 relationship/honorific-lexeme functions + 8 respectful number targets + 4 listening/register checks = 40.
- Grammar and functions: productive subject `-시-` in present, past, and expected future; contrast an addressee-directed `-(으)세요` with a subject-honorific statement/question; limited `계시다`, `드시다`, `주무시다`, `성함`, `연세`, and `말씀` without double honorific marking; ask or state respectful presence, action, schedule, age, name, eating, sleeping, or words.
- Domains and collocations: teacher schedule, older family routine, customer presence or choice, guest reservation, respectful personal information, and ordinary service contact.
- Numbers/counters: respectful people `분`, formal/documentary age `세`, ordinary age `살`, respectful age with `연세` plus justified subject honorification, and established dates/times for a respected subject's schedule. Do not present `세` as honorific by itself.
- Spelling/pronunciation: honorific insertion and contractions, accepted irregular stems before `-시-`, counter spacing, phrase grouping, and question versus instruction contour.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: every card states or makes recoverable who speaks to whom and who the grammatical subject is; never honor the speaker; use titles/kinship terms naturally; Korean 00 formal formulas remain fixed support only.
- Listening: identify the respected subject, distinguish honorific statement/question from instruction, and distinguish respectful person/minute uses of `분` by context.
- Production: choose an ordinary or subject-honorific form, essential lexeme, age form, or title for one justified relationship.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–23.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10; especially conventional formal expressions as unanalyzed chunks, leave-taking directionality, first-meeting context, and request/question contours.
- Leakage exclusions: no productive formal style, broad honorific conversion, humble verbs, business hierarchy, `께서/께` system, self-honorification, or title list padding.

### Slice 25 — integrated basic operational and TOPIK 1 gap closure (`ko-01-0961`–`ko-01-1000`, 40 cards)

- Binding primary-focus allocation: 12 high-risk contrast checks + 10 number/counter integrations + 10 daily-life interaction targets + 8 listening/orthography/register audits = 40.
- Grammar and functions: cumulative integration of every construction in the binding inventory, with explicit gap closure for `은/는` versus `이/가`, `에` versus `에서`, four uses of `있어요/없어요`, three time references, `안` versus `못`, the three request families, the three clause links, ordinary versus subject-honorific forms, and ordinary polite versus inherited fixed formal chunks.
- Domains and collocations: integrated identity, family, home, routine, food, shopping, transport, school, work, calendar, weather, health, invitation, contact, plan, and service repair; no new domain is introduced.
- Numbers/counters: integrate both systems across clock time, dates, prices, ordinary/formal/respectful age, telephone numbers, quantities, routes, floors/rooms, temperature, distance, weight, frequency, and duration; every card combines a function already assigned, not a new numeric variant.
- Spelling/pronunciation: cumulative standard-spelling recovery across all Korean 00 sound families, contractions, irregular forms, particle/counter/request/future/desire spacing, phrase grouping, and statement/question/request/instruction contours.
- Relationships/register/pragmatics: unfamiliar adult, peer, teacher, older family member, customer, and routine staff contexts; actor, subject, title, omission, and honorification must all be recoverable.
- Listening: handle normal careful beginner speech in a complete contextual sentence, including omitted arguments, number grouping, connected sound, tense/polarity, clause relation, and register.
- Production: complete one focused operational target per card across the full TOPIK 1 range and correct a practical misunderstanding without any Korean 02 construction.
- Prerequisite Korean 01 slices: slices 1–24.
- Prerequisite Korean 00 material: all slices 1–10 in full; direct Hangul input, sound/spelling recovery, all forty fixed expressions as support only, phrase grouping, punctuation, and keyboard correction.
- Leakage exclusions: no new construction or lexical niche, no padding, no Korean 00 reuse, no long narration, no later-stage grammar, and no acceptance before every cumulative audit passes.

## Completion accounting and audit

### Range arithmetic

For slice number `n`, the first numeric suffix is `40 × (n - 1) + 1` and the last is `40 × n`. Therefore every range is contiguous, nonoverlapping, and contains exactly 40 IDs.

| Slices | ID coverage | Cards per slice | Slice count | Cards | Principal contribution |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| 1–14 | `ko-01-0001`–`ko-01-0560` | 40 | 14 | 560 | Copula, particles, existence, domains, routines, services, and full practical number/counter foundation |
| 15–20 | `ko-01-0561`–`ko-01-0800` | 40 | 6 | 240 | Descriptions, weather, health, past, future/desire, negation, requests, and instructions |
| 21–23 | `ko-01-0801`–`ko-01-0920` | 40 | 3 | 120 | Short coordination, reason/sequence, contrast, and connector control |
| 24 | `ko-01-0921`–`ko-01-0960` | 40 | 1 | 40 | Productive basic subject honorification and essential respectful forms |
| 25 | `ko-01-0961`–`ko-01-1000` | 40 | 1 | 40 | Integrated high-risk contrasts, TOPIK 1 gap closure, and cumulative audit |
| **Total** | `ko-01-0001`–`ko-01-1000` | **40** | **25** | **1,000** | **Complete Korean 01 matrix** |

### Binding audit gates

- **Ranges and arithmetic:** all 25 ranges must match the formula above; IDs must be sequential with no gap or overlap; each accepted slice adds exactly 40 rows to the accepted-primary-target index.
- **Construction assignments:** every row in the construction inventory must have accepted evidence in an assigned slice. A later revisit cannot compensate for a missing introduction unless the matrix is revised before generation.
- **High-risk contrasts:** every row in the high-risk map requires cards whose situations distinguish both sides; a label or ending-only pair is insufficient.
- **Number/counter coverage:** every system, function, counter, and unit row requires at least one natural accepted use in an assigned slice and a cumulative slice-25 audit. Mechanical digit variants do not satisfy separate rows.
- **Prerequisite order:** slice 1 uses Korean 00 only; each later slice uses all and only accepted earlier Korean 01 material. No later construction may leak backward as support.
- **Register and honorification:** all person cards with a form-sensitive relationship identify speaker, listener, and subject; `-시-`, titles, age forms, and conventional formal chunks receive their assigned contrast audits.
- **Pronunciation and orthography:** each slice audits standard spacing and at least its assigned sound context; all answers retain underlying standard spelling.
- **Originality and duplication:** Korean 00 and cumulative Korean 01 comparisons must report zero exact, normalized, semantic, and mechanical duplicate primary targets before a slice is accepted.
- **Card contract:** each sentence contains its cloze exactly once; answer equals cloze; punctuation remains visible and outside the target; no cloze crosses punctuation or speaker turns; audio makes the answer fair.
- **Leakage:** each slice's local exclusions and the stage-wide exclusions are binding. A useful sentence containing a reserved Korean 02 construction is still rejected.

After slice 25, run separate fresh cumulative reviews for: particles and omitted subjects; present/past/future and negation; requests/instructions/desire; the complete number/counter inventory; high-frequency irregulars; subject honorification and relationship context; ordinary polite register and Korean 02 leakage; spelling, spacing, connected sound, and question intonation; cloze boundaries and answer fairness; and exact, normalized, semantic, and mechanical duplication. Confirmed defects are corrected in the source cards, not recorded in a review artifact.

### Why exactly 1,000 cards completes coverage without padding

The count follows the coverage work: 560 cards establish the full beginner grammar, particle, daily-domain, service, and practical number/counter base; 240 give substantive contextual control of descriptions, health needs, time reference, polarity, desire, requests, and instructions; 120 establish the three permitted clause relations; 40 isolate relationship-sensitive subject honorification; and 40 test cumulative operational integration and close documented gaps. Each 40-card slice has disjoint primary-focus quotas and acceptance requires one unique target per card.

No quota is filled by reciting a paradigm, changing a name or numeral, copying Korean 00, or hiding a larger duplicate span. Conversely, reaching `ko-01-1000` does not complete the stage if any construction, number/counter function, domain, high-risk contrast, listening target, production target, register context, sound/spelling assignment, or prerequisite audit lacks accepted evidence. This makes 1,000 the complete planned coverage set rather than a padded endpoint.
